Q1. A takes 6 days to finish a work and B takes 10 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 6 days
B. 3 3/4 days
C. 16 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 3 3/4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(6,10)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/6=5,\quad B=30/10=3
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{5+3}=3.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{6\times 10}{6+10}=3.75
\]
Answer: 3 3/4 days

---

Q2. A team has 6 men and 8 women. They finish 4, 3 boxes per person per day respectively. How many days are needed for 240 boxes?
A. 5 days
B. 3 3/4 days
C. 10 days
D. 4 days
Answer: 5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_group_conversion_and_or
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{240}{6\times 4+8\times 3}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 5 days

---

Q3. A work requires 10 workers for 15 days with 8 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 20 days with 6 hours per day?
A. 14 workers
B. 15 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 13 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
10\times 15\times 8=M_2\times 20\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

---

Q4. A and B have efficiencies in the ratio 3:4. If they finish a job together in 12 days, how many days will A alone take?
A. 9 days
B. 16 days
C. 28 days
D. 19 days
Answer: 28 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(3+4)\times 12
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{84}{3}=28
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(3+4)12}{3}=28
\]
Answer: 28 days

---

Q5. Two workers work for equal time. Their wages are ₹800 and ₹1200. What is the ratio of work done by A and B?
A. 2000:1200
B. 1200:800
C. 80:60
D. 2:3
Answer: 2:3
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_efficiency_from_wages
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W_A:W_B=E_A:E_B
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
E_A:E_B=800:1200
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
E_A:E_B=2:3
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=2:3
\]
Answer: 2:3

---

Q6. Workers A, B and C work one after another daily. A repairs 10 metres of road, B 5 metres and C 3 metres. How many days are needed for 72 metres?
A. 18 days
B. 12 days
C. 6 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 12 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=10+5+3
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 days

---

Q7. A+B+C finish a job in 10 days, while A+B finish the same job in 12 days. In how many days can C finish it alone?
A. 60 days
B. 64 days
C. 90 days
D. 62 days
Answer: 60 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_pairwise_total_abc
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_X=r_{team}-\sum r_i
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
r_X=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{12}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_X=60
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 60 days

---

Q8. Starting with A, two workers alternate. A completes 6 records and B completes 4 records on their respective turns. How many hours will be required to complete 120 records?
A. 24 hours

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+4
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 24 hours

Q9. 720 ration packets are available. Daily consumption is 36 packets. For how many days will the ration last?
A. 18 days
B. 24 days
C. 20 days
D. 25 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_resource_rate_equivalence
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
D=\frac{720}{36}
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=\frac{S}{R}
\]
Answer: 20 days

---

Q10. A with B takes 10 days, B with C takes 12 days and C with A takes 15 days. How many days will A alone take?
A. 12 days
B. 25 days
C. 30 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 24 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_pairwise_ab_bc_ac
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
2(A+B+C)=(A+B)+(B+C)+(C+A)
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
AB=10,\ BC=12,\ CA=15
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=24
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
A=\frac{AB+AC-BC}{2}
\]
Answer: 24 days

---

Q11. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 10:20. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?
A. 30:20
B. 10:20
C. 2:1
D. 20:30
Answer: 2:1
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_time_ratio_from_efficiency
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T_A:T_B=E_B:E_A
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T_A:T_B=20:10
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A:T_B=2:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2:1
\]
Answer: 2:1

---

Q12. A, B and C are printers with hourly page counts 12, 15 and 18. If all work for 5 hours, what is the output in pages?
A. 168.75 pages
B. 220 pages
C. 225 pages
D. 228 pages
Answer: 225 pages
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_printer_job_queue
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
O=\sum r_it_i
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
O=12\times 5+15\times 5+18\times 5
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
O=225
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
O=(r_1+r_2+r_3)t
\]
Answer: 225 pages

---

Q13. 2 men, 3 women and 4 children work together on 240 boxes. Their daily capacities are 4, 2, 1 boxes respectively. How many days will they take?
A. 13 1/3 days
B. 8 1/3 days
C. 11 1/3 days
D. 10 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_worker_equivalence_men_women_children
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
T=\frac{W}{\sum ne}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
T=\frac{240}{2\times 4+3\times 2+4\times 1}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=W/R
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Q14. A team completes 15 files per day for 2 days and then 24 files per day after a team change. How many days are required for 120 files?
A. 11 3/4 days
B. 4 3/5 days
C. 4 3/4 days
D. 5 3/4 days
Answer: 5 3/4 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=15\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{90}{24}=5.75
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 5 3/4 days

---

Q16. A filling pipe delivers 12 litres of water per minute for 40 minutes. How many litres of water are delivered?
A. 481 litres
B. 480 litres
C. 476 litres
D. 720 litres
Answer: 480 litres
Difficulty: Easy
Family: pc_tank_capacity_from_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
C=rt
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
C=12\times 40
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
C=480
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
C=r\times t
\]
Answer: 480 litres

Q17. A works alone for 3 days on a job. A's time is 12 days and B's time is 24 days. How many days are needed in all if B joins afterward?
A. 9 days
B. 7 days
C. 11 1/4 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=10\times 3,\quad R=120-30
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=3+\frac{90}{10+5}=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3+\frac{120-10\times 3}{10+5}
\]
Answer: 9 days

---

Q18. A pipe starts filling a tank and works alone for 2 hours. A leak that empties a full tank in 30 hours then starts. If the pipe alone fills in 12 hours, in how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 18 2/3 hours
B. 16 2/3 hours
C. 28 hours
D. 14 2/3 hours
Answer: 18 2/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_drain_after_partial_fill
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_f-r_e}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
r=1/12-1/30
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=18\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W_l}{r_n}
\]
Answer: 18 2/3 hours

Q20. If A can complete a work in 15 days and B can complete it in 21 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?

B. 15 days
C. 36 days
D. 8 3/4 days
Answer: 8 3/4 days

Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(15,21)=105
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=105/15=7,\quad B=105/21=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{105}{7+5}=8.75
\]

\[
T=\frac{15\times 21}{15+21}=8.75

Q21. 12 workers working 8 hours per day finish the job in 10 days. If the daily working time is 6 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 16 days?
A. 19 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 10 workers
D. 12 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_road_construction_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
12\times 10\times 8=M_2\times 16\times 6
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{12\times 10\times 8}{16\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q24. A pipe takes 12 hours to fill a tank from empty. The tank is already one-fourth full. In how many hours will it be completely filled?
A. 13 1/2 hours
B. 9 hours
C. 13 hours
D. 11 hours
Answer: 9 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_partial_tank_initially_filled
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
T=\frac{1-f}{r_f}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
T=\frac{1-1/4}{1/12}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=(1-f)F
\]
Answer: 9 hours

Q25. A team of 8 workers has completed 48 items from a 360-item order. With 12 days left, how many workers must be added?
A. 16 workers
B. 22.5 workers
C. 24 workers
D. 18 workers
Answer: 18 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{312}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=18
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 18 workers

---

Q26. Two workers receive ₹12000 for a job. Their contributions are proportional to 8:12. How much will A get?
A. ₹6000
B. ₹4806
C. ₹4803
D. ₹4800
Answer: ₹4800
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_wage_partial_time
Explanation:
Convert each person's work into contribution units.
\[
Share=\frac{c_i}{\sum c}\times W
\]
Use the work-share ratio for the wage.
\[
Share=\frac{8}{20}\times 12000
\]
Apply the wage or contract amount.
\[
Share=4800
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
Share=contribution\ ratio\times wage
\]
Answer: ₹4800

---

Q27. A food stock lasts 30 days for 20 people. How many days will it last for 30 people?
A. 20 days
B. 15 days
C. 22 days
D. 25 days
Answer: 20 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:
Convert the stock into consumption units.
\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
20\times 30=30\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=20
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{20\times 30}{30}
\]
Answer: 20 days

Q28. A filling pipe and a drain work alternately for one hour each. The pipe adds 12 litres and the drain removes 5 litres. How many hours are needed for a 60-litre tank?
A. 24 1/2 hours
B. 16 1/3 hours
C. 12 1/3 hours
D. 19 1/3 hours
Answer: 16 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Hard
Family: pc_alternating_fill_empty
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
W_{cycle}=12-5
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
60\ units,\ cycle=7
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
T=16\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=C_f+t_f
\]
Answer: 16 1/3 hours

Q33. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 4:5. Together they complete a work in 10 days. In how many days can A alone complete the work?

Answer: 22 1/2 days

Family: tw_efficiency_ratio_scaling

\[
W=(a+b)T
\]

\[
W=(4+5)\times 10
\]

\[
T_A=\frac{90}{4}=22.5
\]

\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(4+5)10}{4}=22.5
\]

Q35. A work requires 18 workers for 8 days with 5 working hours per day. How many workers will be needed for 12 days with 6 hours per day?
A. 10 workers
B. 13 workers
C. 18 workers
D. 27 workers
Answer: 10 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_man_days_hours_basic
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
18\times 8\times 5=M_2\times 12\times 6
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
M_2=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{18\times 8\times 5}{12\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q36. 10 workers were expected to complete a job in the planned time. After working for 6 days, 4 workers left. By how many days will the job be delayed?

\[
D=\frac{180}{6}-\frac{180}{10}

Q37. A and B together finish a job in 10 days. A alone takes 18 days. How many days will B alone take?
A. 14 days
B. 22 1/2 days
C. 8 days
D. 28 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_individual_from_combined
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{18}
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
B=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{10\times 18}{18-10}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q40. If a work is completed in 10 days and 4 days have passed, what fraction of the work is yet to be done?

Q42. A tank has two filling pipes. Pipe A fills it in 12 hours and pipe B in 18 hours. Pipe A is closed after 2 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled?
A. 12 hours
B. 15 hours
C. 21 hours
D. 17 hours
Answer: 15 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_pipe_closed_before_completion
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=(r_A+r_B)t_1,\quad T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=(3+2)\times 2
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{26}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_B}
\]
Answer: 15 hours

Q43. A and B can separately complete a work in 10 and 20 days. A is absent for the last 2 days and B is absent for the last 3 days. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 7 days
B. 13 days
C. 7 1/5 days
D. 9 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
18(T-2)+9(T-3)=180
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days

\]

Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(8,12)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/8=3,\quad B=24/12=2
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{3+2}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{8\times 12}{8+12}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

---

Q113. 6 men, 8 women work together on 360 boxes. Their daily capacities are 4, 3 boxes respectively. How many days will they take?

Q114. 15 workers working 6 hours per day finish the job in 16 days. If the daily working time is 8 hours, how many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days?
A. 15 workers
B. 18 workers
C. 20 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_painting_walls_area_work
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q115. The efficiency ratio of A to B is 2:3. They complete the work together in 6 days. In how many days can A do it alone?
A. 4 days
B. 15 days
C. 9 days
D. 11 days
Answer: 15 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_relative_efficiency_percent
Explanation:

\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(2+3)\times 6
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{30}{2}=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(2+3)6}{2}=15
\]
Answer: 15 days

Q117. The order is A, then B, then C, and it repeats. They build 6, 5 and 5 metres of wall on their turns. How many days are needed for 90 metres?
A. 16 4/5 days
B. 25 1/5 days
C. 20 4/5 days
D. 18 4/5 days
Answer: 16 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:
Find the work completed in one full cycle.
\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i
\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[
W_{cycle}=6+5+5
\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=16.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 16 4/5 days

Q118. A, B and C together take 8 days. A and B together take 20 days. In how many days can C alone complete it?

\]

\[
T_X=1/r_X
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

\]

\]

Q132. 12 workers have packed 96 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 8 days, how many additional workers are needed?

\[

\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{144}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 6 workers

Q133. The total wage for a job is ₹7200. A and B contribute in the ratio 8:12. How much should A receive?

\[

\]

\[

\]

\[

\]
Answer: ₹2880

Q149. Construction progresses by 15 metres daily and damage reduces it by 3 metres daily. How many days are required for 180 metres?

\[

Difficulty: Medium

Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
W=(a+b)T
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W=(4+5)\times 10
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A=\frac{90}{4}=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T_A=\frac{(a+b)T}{a}=\frac{(4+5)10}{4}=22.5
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

---

Q188. A and B earn ₹300 and ₹500 for the same duration of work. What is the ratio of work done by A and B?

Q189. The order is A, then B, then C, and it repeats. They build 6, 4 and 4 metres of wall on their turns. How many days are needed for 60 metres?

Answer: 12 2/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:

\[
W_{cycle}=\sum r_i

\[

\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=12\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 12 2/3 days

Q203. Normally a pipe fills a tank in 10 hours, but with leakage it fills in 15 hours. What is the emptying time of the leak?
A. 31 hours
B. 24 hours
C. 30 hours
D. 34 hours
Answer: 30 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{10}-\frac{1}{15}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=30
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 30 hours

Q217. 10 farm workers can harvest a field in 15 days by working 8 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 20 days by working 6 hours a day?

Explanation:

\[

\]

\[

\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=10
\]

\[
M_2=\frac{10\times 15\times 8}{20\times 6}=10
\]
Answer: 10 workers

Q218. If A can complete a work in 10 days and B can complete it in 20 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?

\]

\]

\[
T=\frac{20}{2+1}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]

\[
T=\frac{10\times 20}{10+20}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

Q228. A alone finishes a job in 6 days, while B alone finishes it in 10 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

Q265. A and B work on alternate days, starting with A. A packs 8 boxes in a day and B packs 4 boxes in a day. If 72 boxes are to be packed, in how many days will the job be finished?

Q266. A and B work together at first. A's time is 10 days and B's time is 30 days. If A leaves after 2 days, when will the job be finished in all?

\]

\[

\]
Answer: 24 days

Q269. One pipe fills a tank in 12 hours, while another empties it in 36 hours. In how many hours will the tank be completely filled with both open?
A. 13 1/3 hours
B. 19 1/3 hours
C. 16 1/3 hours
D. 16 2/3 hours
Answer: 13 1/3 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_find_pipe_rate_from_net_time
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{T}-\frac{1}{A}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{B}=\frac{1}{8}-\frac{1}{20}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
B=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
B=\frac{AT}{A-T}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 hours

Q270. 15 farm workers can harvest a field in 16 days by working 6 hours a day. How many workers are needed to finish it in 12 days by working 8 hours a day?
A. 18 workers
B. 20 workers
C. 15 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 15 workers
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_farm_harvest_workers
Explanation:
Write output as rate multiplied by active time.
\[
M_1D_1H_1=M_2D_2H_2
\]
Add the outputs of all active agents.
\[
15\times 16\times 6=M_2\times 12\times 8
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
M_2=15
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
M_2=\frac{15\times 16\times 6}{12\times 8}=15
\]
Answer: 15 workers

Q271. If A can complete a work in 15 days and B can complete it in 30 days, how many days are needed when both work at the same time?
A. 10 days
B. 22 1/2 days
C. 45 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 10 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,30)=30
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=30/15=2,\quad B=30/30=1
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{30}{2+1}=10
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 30}{15+30}=10
\]
Answer: 10 days

T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6 4/5 days

Q299. A alone takes 8 days and B alone takes 24 days. A works alone for 2 days, then B joins. How many days will the job take in all?
A. 6 1/2 days
B. 3 1/2 days
C. 10 1/2 days
D. 9 3/4 days
Answer: 6 1/2 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=15\times 2,\quad R=120-30
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{90}{15+5}=6.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{120-15\times 2}{15+5}
\]
Answer: 6 1/2 days

Q300. A takes 24 days to finish a work and B takes 6 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 30 days
B. 15 days
C. 4 4/5 days
D. 6 days
Answer: 4 4/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,6)=24
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=24/24=1,\quad B=24/6=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{24}{1+4}=4.8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 6}{24+6}=4.8
\]
Answer: 4 4/5 days

Q335. A can complete a piece of work in 15 days and B can complete the same work in 21 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 36 days
B. 18 days
C. 8 3/4 days
D. 15 days
Answer: 8 3/4 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[

\[

\]

\[
T=\frac{105}{7+5}=8.75
\]

Q340. A packing job has 24 boxes. The team packs 3 boxes daily at first for 5 days, and then 9 boxes daily. How many days are needed in all?
A. 9 days
B. 11 days
C. 6 days
D. 7 1/5 days
Answer: 6 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_multi_phase_join_leave
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
\sum r_it_i=W
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=3\times 5
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=5+\frac{9}{9}=6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=t_1+\frac{W-W_1}{r_2}
\]
Answer: 6 days

Q341. A can do a work in 10 days and B in 30 days. After A works for 2 days alone, both work together. In how many days is the work completed?
A. 6 2/5 days
B. 14 days
C. 7 days
D. 8 days
Answer: 8 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=12\times 2,\quad R=120-24
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=2+\frac{96}{12+4}=8
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=2+\frac{120-12\times 2}{12+4}
\]
Answer: 8 days

Q342. A takes 24 days to finish a work and B takes 30 days for the same work. In how many days can A and B finish it together?
A. 27 days
B. 13 1/3 days
C. 54 days
D. 24 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(24,30)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/24=5,\quad B=120/30=4
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{5+4}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{24\times 30}{24+30}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

\]
Count full cycles before the last turn.
\[

\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[

\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 12 days

Q376. An inlet pipe fills a tank in 15 hours. With a leak, the same tank fills in 25 hours. In how many hours can the leak empty a full tank?
A. 34 1/2 hours
B. 33 1/2 hours
C. 45 hours
D. 37 1/2 hours
Answer: 37 1/2 hours
Difficulty: Medium
Family: pc_leak_hidden_rate
Explanation:
Write filling rates as positive and emptying rates as negative.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{F}-\frac{1}{T}
\]
Find the effective tank rate.
\[
\frac{1}{L}=\frac{1}{15}-\frac{1}{25}
\]
Divide the required tank work by that rate.
\[
L=37.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
L=\frac{FT}{T-F}
\]
Answer: 37 1/2 hours

Q388. A can complete a piece of work in 30 days and B can complete the same work in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it working together?
A. 13 1/3 days
B. 24 days
C. 27 days
D. 54 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_basic_combined_work
Explanation:

\[
W=LCM(30,24)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/30=4,\quad B=120/24=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{4+5}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 24}{30+24}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

Subtract the work already completed.
\[
R=360-8\times 10
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
D=\frac{280}{6}-\frac{280}{8}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D=T_a-T_p
\]
Answer: 11 2/3 days

Q410. A and B together can complete a work in 12 days. A alone can complete it in 20 days. In how many days will B alone complete it?

\[

\]

Q418. A pipe fills a tank in 12 hours. A leak slows the filling to 22 hours. What time will the leak take to empty the tank alone?

Q422. A starts a work and B joins after 4 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 8 and 12 days. What is the total time taken?

Answer: 6 2/5 days

\[

\]

\[

\[

\]

Q423. A alone finishes a job in 20 days, while B alone finishes it in 30 days. How many days will the job take if both work together?

\[

\]

\[

\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{3+2}=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{20\times 30}{20+30}=12
\]
Answer: 12 days

Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(15,12)=60
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=60/15=4,\quad B=60/12=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{60}{4+5}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{15\times 12}{15+12}=6\frac{2}{3}
\]
Answer: 6 2/3 days

---

Q447. Workers A, B and C work one after another daily. A repairs 8 metres of road, B 6 metres and C 4 metres. How many days are needed for 120 metres?

D. 29 1/2 days
Answer: 19 2/3 days

Family: tw_conditional_activation
Explanation:

\[

\[

\]
Add only the needed part of the final turn.
\[
T=19\frac{2}{3}
\]

\[
T=cycles+final\ turn
\]
Answer: 19 2/3 days

Q449. The efficiencies of A and B are in the ratio 24:10. What is the ratio of the times taken by A and B for the same work?

Answer: 5:12

\[
T_A:T_B=10:24

T=5:12
\]

Q452. After a delay, 80 of 180 records are completed by 8 workers. How many extra workers are needed if the remaining records must be finished in 8 days?
A. 7 workers
B. 5 workers
C. 4 workers
D. 11 workers
Answer: 5 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{100}{1\times 8}\right\rceil-8
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 5 workers

Q458. A alone takes 10 days and B alone takes 15 days. During a joint job, A does not work in the last 2 days and B does not work in the last 3 days. What is the total time?
A. 8 2/5 days
B. 12 2/5 days
C. 5 2/5 days
D. 12 3/5 days
Answer: 8 2/5 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[

\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[

\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=8.4
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 8 2/5 days

\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T_A:T_B=3:2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=3:2
\]
Answer: 3:2

Q484. A starts a work and B joins after 4 days. A and B can finish the work alone in 10 and 15 days. What is the total time taken?
A. 7 3/5 days
B. 9 1/2 days
C. 9 3/5 days
D. 2 3/5 days
Answer: 7 3/5 days
Difficulty: Medium
Family: tw_delayed_join
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
W_1=r_At_1,\ T_2=\frac{W-W_1}{r_A+r_B}
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
W_1=6\times 4,\quad R=60-24
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
T=4+\frac{36}{6+4}=7.6
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=4+\frac{60-6\times 4}{6+4}
\]
Answer: 7 3/5 days

Q485. One worker can do a job in 30 days and another can do it in 24 days. In how many days will they complete it together?
A. 24 days
B. 27 days
C. 13 1/3 days
D. 54 days
Answer: 13 1/3 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_lcm_hidden_total_work
Explanation:
Take the LCM as total work.
\[
W=LCM(30,24)=120
\]
Find each worker's one-period efficiency.
\[
A=120/30=4,\quad B=120/24=5
\]
Divide total work by the combined efficiency.
\[
T=\frac{120}{4+5}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{30\times 24}{30+24}=13\frac{1}{3}
\]
Answer: 13 1/3 days

---

Q486. If A's time is 10 days and B's time is 20 days, what ratio of work will they do in equal time?

\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
W_A:W_B=20:10
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
W_A:W_B=2:1
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
E=2:1
\]
Answer: 2:1

Q487. 12 workers have packed 72 boxes out of 240. To finish the rest in 12 days, how many additional workers are needed?
A. 0.5 workers
B. 2 workers
C. 5 workers
D. 3 workers
Answer: 2 workers
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_contract_deadline_extra_workers
Explanation:
Split the work into timeline phases.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{R}{r\times t}\right\rceil-N_c
\]
Subtract the work already completed.
\[
N_{extra}=\left\lceil\frac{168}{1\times 12}\right\rceil-12
\]
Use the active rate for the remaining phase.
\[
N_{extra}=2
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
N_{extra}=N_r-N_c
\]
Answer: 2 workers

Q488. Food is sufficient for 30 people for 30 days. For how many days will the same food last for 40 people?
A. 20 1/2 days
B. 27 1/2 days
C. 27 days
D. 22 1/2 days
Answer: 22 1/2 days
Difficulty: Easy
Family: tw_food_resource_basic
Explanation:

\[
M_1D_1=M_2D_2
\]
Subtract the stock already consumed.
\[
30\times 30=40\times D_2
\]
Divide the remaining stock by the new daily use.
\[
D_2=22.5
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
D_2=\frac{30\times 30}{40}
\]
Answer: 22 1/2 days

Q489. A team adds 15 metres to a project each day, but 5 metres are lost each day. What is the time for 120 metres?

D. 15 days

\]

\[
T=\frac{120}{15-5}
\]
Simplify the total output.
\[
T=12
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[

\]
Answer: 12 days

Q492. In a joint job, A alone needs 10 days and B alone needs 20 days. A is not present in the last 2 days and B in the last 3 days. How many days does the job take?
A. 10 days
B. 4 days
C. 9 days
D. 6 3/4 days
Answer: 9 days
Difficulty: Hard
Family: tw_backward_leave
Explanation:
Write the rate relation used in this question.
\[
r_A(T-a)+r_B(T-b)=W
\]
Substitute the numbers from the statement.
\[
26(T-2)+13(T-3)=260
\]
Simplify the relation to get the answer.
\[
T=9
\]
Shortcut / Exam Method:
\[
T=\frac{W+A\times 2+B\times 3}{A+B}
\]
Answer: 9 days
